### Added (handbook/frontend/react-hooks-useeffect-and-useref.md — F-105–106, second frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (no ambiguity — F-105/F-106 are the next entries after F-101–104, so proceeded directly without an `AskUserQuestion` round).
- `handbook/frontend/react-hooks-useeffect-and-useref.md` (F-105 `useEffect`: dependency arrays, cleanup, common footguns; F-106 `useRef`: DOM access, mutable values) — Junior/Mid depth per the Scope Addendum.
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-hooks/`, 5 demo components. Verified live via browser automation: console confirmed clean on a fresh tab, `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build (565ms, 3 output files).
- **Genuine bug caught and fixed while building the demo, not hypothetical:** an initial `RefMutableValueDemo` used a dependency-less `useEffect` calling `setState` to count renders — this is a real infinite-loop pattern, reproduced live as a "Maximum update depth exceeded" console error. Fixed by moving the render counter into the render body via a plain ref mutation instead of an effect; documented in the demo's own code comment and in the chapter's Core Concepts section as real content, not silently corrected.
- **Two genuine, unplanned findings surfaced during verification, both preserved as real content:** (1) toggling a leaky (no-cleanup) `setInterval` ticker "on" exactly 2 times produced 4 real leaked intervals, not 2 — traced to React 19's `<StrictMode>` (the Vite template default) intentionally double-invoking effect mount/cleanup in development specifically to surface missing-cleanup bugs fast; a clean ticker with proper cleanup showed 0 residual regardless of toggle count, confirmed against the same module-level ground-truth counter. (2) A render counter (incremented directly in the render body) jumped by 2, not 1, after a single genuine state update — a second, distinct StrictMode behavior: React also double-invokes a component's render function body per commit in development to help surface non-pure renders.
- **The stale-closure bug reproduced and measured, not just described:** after 3 real clicks settling `count` at 2, a buggy logger (empty-deps effect closing over mount-time state) showed `[0, 0, 0, 0, 0]` while a ref-based fixed version showed `[2, 2, 2, 2, 2]` — captured via direct DOM reads.
- **The DOM-access claim verified directly:** after clicking "Focus the input," `document.activeElement` was confirmed via JS inspection to be the actual ref-targeted `<input>` node.
- Added a `react-hooks` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5184, alongside the existing `react-fundamentals` entry on 5183).
- Cross-linked to `react-fundamentals-jsx-components-props-and-state.md` (prerequisite) and `00-project/frontend-topic-register.md`.
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-usememo-usecallback-and-usecontext.md — F-107–108, third frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-107/F-108 are the next entries after F-105/F-106, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round).
- `handbook/frontend/react-usememo-usecallback-and-usecontext.md` (F-107 `useMemo`/`useCallback`: what they actually prevent, when they don't help; F-108 `useContext` and the Context API) — Junior/Mid depth per the Scope Addendum.
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-memo-and-context/`, 4 demo components. Verified live via browser automation: console confirmed clean on a fresh tab, `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **`useMemo` recompute counts measured, not asserted:** after 2 unrelated-state clicks then 1 dependency-changing click, a memoized computation's ground-truth counter moved from 2→4 (only on the relevant click) while an unmemoized version moved from 2→8 (on every render). `React.memo` defeated by an inline handler measured the same way: 2→6 (memo provided zero benefit) vs. 2→2 (unchanged) for a `useCallback`-stabilized handler.
- **The chapter's centerpiece — a genuine modeling mistake caught by running the demo, not corrected silently:** the first draft of `ContextRerenderCostDemo` memoized no consumers. Running it produced a real result contradicting the intended lesson — incrementing an unrelated `count` re-rendered every consumer in BOTH a combined-context and a "split-context" scenario, making the context-splitting fix look like it did nothing. Diagnosed: with no `memo()` anywhere, every descendant re-renders whenever its parent re-renders, entirely independent of Context — the demo was never testing what it claimed to test. Fixed by wrapping every consumer in `memo()`; re-run showed the intended result cleanly: combined-context `FlagConsumer` climbed 2→4 (its context value is a new object reference whenever `count` changes, bypassing `memo`), while the split-context `FlagConsumer` (subscribing only to its own unchanged value) stayed at 2 — unaffected. Both the broken first attempt and the fix are preserved in the demo file's own comment, the chapter's Core Concepts section, and the practice app's README, not smoothed over.
- Added a `react-memo-and-context` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5185).
- Cross-linked to `react-hooks-useeffect-and-useref.md` (prerequisite) and `00-project/frontend-topic-register.md`.
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-usereducer-and-custom-hooks.md — F-109–110, fourth frontend chapter, closes the React Hooks cluster)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-109/F-110 are the next entries after F-107/F-108, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Closes out the full "React Hooks" cluster (F-105 through F-110) started two chapters ago.
- `handbook/frontend/react-usereducer-and-custom-hooks.md` (F-109 `useReducer`, when it beats `useState`; F-110 custom hooks: design, naming, composition rules) — Junior/Mid depth per the Scope Addendum.
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-reducer-and-custom-hooks/`, 4 demo components + 2 custom hook files. Verified live via browser automation: console confirmed clean on a fresh tab, `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **A real, reproduced incomplete-reset bug:** a `useState`-based form's reset handler, written before a `phone` field was added, left `phone: "555-0100"` behind after clicking Reset; the `useReducer` version's `RESET` action (`return initialFormState`) correctly cleared it, since reset there has no separate field list to have forgotten.
- **A deterministic (not timing-dependent) stale-derivation bug:** rather than relying on click-speed timing — unreliable to control precisely via browser automation — the bug was made deterministic by calling the increment logic twice inside one handler, guaranteeing staleness on the first click every time. Real captured result: `useState` version showed `count=2` but `lastAction="incremented to 1"` (wrong — both calls read the same stale closure); `useReducer` version showed `count=2, lastAction="incremented to 2"` (correct — each dispatch computes from the true latest state).
- **Custom hook instance isolation verified directly:** two independent `useToggle` panels — toggling Panel A produced `Panel A: OPEN, Panel B: CLOSED`, unaffected.
- **`useDebouncedValue` — a genuinely useful custom hook, all three lifecycle states captured in one real sequence, not just before/after:** immediate raw-value update after typing; a mid-flight read with zero wait (via a native input-value setter to control timing precisely) showing the debounced value still holding the PREVIOUS value, genuinely lagging; then, after a 1-second wait, the debounced value catching up and the commit counter moving from 1 to 2 — proving a burst of rapid changes produces exactly one debounced commit.
- Added a `react-reducer-and-custom-hooks` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5186).
- Cross-linked to `react-usememo-usecallback-and-usecontext.md` (prerequisite) and `00-project/frontend-topic-register.md`.
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-component-patterns.md — F-111, fifth frontend chapter, first Advanced-tier entry)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-111 is the next entry after F-110, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Given its breadth (four genuinely distinct patterns), taken as its own standalone chapter rather than grouped.
- `handbook/frontend/react-component-patterns.md` (F-111: composition vs. inheritance, render props, compound components, HOCs) — Mid/Senior depth, the frontend domain's first Advanced-tier chapter, following the Junior/Mid React Hooks cluster (F-105–110).
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-component-patterns/`, 4 demo components + 1 custom hook file. Verified live via browser automation, including real clicks and real window resizes. `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **A real automation-tool limitation caught and worked around, not hidden:** `resize_window` changes the CDP viewport metrics (confirmed correct via `window.innerWidth` reading the new value through direct JS) but does not dispatch a native `resize` DOM event — none of the three window-width demo components' listeners fired on their own. A real user dragging a browser window does fire `resize`; this is an automation-tooling artifact, not a bug in any of the three implementations. Worked around by dispatching a synthetic `window.dispatchEvent(new Event('resize'))`, after which all three implementations (HOC, render prop, custom hook) updated correctly and identically across two separate resizes (800px, then 1200px) — proving functional equivalence.
- **Composition vs. inheritance** verified with one `Panel` component rendering two specializations via props alone (`tone="alert"`/`tone="info"`), no subclass anywhere in the file.
- **Compound components** verified with a real, clickable Tabs widget (`Tabs`/`Tabs.List`/`Tabs.Tab`/`Tabs.Panel`) — clicking "Settings" then "Billing" correctly switched the rendered panel content each time, with the caller's JSX never referencing the shared `activeTab` state directly.
- Added a `react-component-patterns` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5187).
- Cross-linked to `react-usereducer-and-custom-hooks.md` (prerequisite) and `react-usememo-usecallback-and-usecontext.md` (shared Context re-render-cost model, since compound components are Context under a naming convention).
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-reconciliation-and-fiber.md — F-112, sixth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-112 is the next entry after F-111, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Second Advanced-tier chapter, internals-depth, mirroring the JVM-internals treatment on the backend side.
- `handbook/frontend/react-reconciliation-and-fiber.md` (F-112: how React actually diffs/schedules) — deliberately scoped to synchronous reconciliation only (diffing, DOM node identity, batching); the scheduling/priority side of Fiber is left to Concurrent React (F-113), where it can be demonstrated with real concurrent-feature APIs instead of described abstractly.
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-reconciliation-and-fiber/`, 3 demo components. Verified live via browser automation, including direct DOM node object-identity checks. `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **DOM node reuse proven by direct `===` identity comparison, not just visual persistence:** typed `"persist-me"` into an input, clicked "Re-render" twice — the text never lost, and a real identity check confirmed 3 renders where the captured DOM node was the exact same object reference as the previous render's, not merely similar-looking.
- **A real, reproduced contrast between type-change (state lost) and prop-change (state preserved) at the same JSX position:** incremented a nested counter to 2, then toggled the wrapping element's TYPE (`<div>` → `<section>`) — counter reset to 0 (React destroyed and rebuilt the subtree). Incremented again to 2, then toggled only a PROP on the same type — counter stayed at 2, unaffected.
- **Batching proven by a real commit counter, not asserted:** a handler calling 3 separate `setState` functions moved the commit count by exactly `+2` (one real commit, doubled by StrictMode) — the identical increase produced by a second handler calling only 1 `setState` function, direct proof that multiple updates in one handler cost the same single commit as one update.
- Added a `react-reconciliation-and-fiber` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5188).
- Cross-linked to `react-component-patterns.md` (prerequisite) and `react-fundamentals-jsx-components-props-and-state.md` (the index-as-key bug is this chapter's DOM-node-reuse mechanism applied incorrectly).
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-concurrent-rendering.md — F-113, seventh frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-113 is the next entry after F-112, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Third Advanced-tier chapter — the scheduling/priority half of Fiber that F-112 explicitly deferred here.
- `handbook/frontend/react-concurrent-rendering.md` (F-113: transitions, `useDeferredValue`, `useTransition`, Suspense for data) — covers what makes rendering interruptible/prioritizable, building directly on F-112's Fiber structure.
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-concurrent/`, 3 demo components. Verified live via browser automation, including a real observed Suspense fallback window. `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **`useTransition` proven with a real `isPending` history, not a claim:** typing `"item-5"` into a 20,000-item filtered-list demo produced `pending log: pending: true -> pending: false`, direct evidence the low-priority filter update was deprioritized without blocking the urgent input.
- **`useDeferredValue` proven the same way:** typing `"entry-5"` produced `stale log: stale: true -> stale: false`, with `query`/`deferredQuery` converging once the expensive filter caught up.
- **Suspense + `use()` proven with an actually observed fallback window, not inferred from docs:** sequential browser-automation tool calls were slower than the original 800ms simulated fetch delay, so every check landed post-resolution — worked around by raising the delay to 3000ms and combining the click and DOM poll into one `javascript_exec` call. Real captured result: `+100ms → "Loading user..."`, `+3.2s → "Loaded: User 2 (id 2)"`.
- Added a `react-concurrent` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5189).
- Cross-linked to `react-reconciliation-and-fiber.md` (prerequisite) and `react-usereducer-and-custom-hooks.md` (the stale-closure concern this chapter's batching-adjacent material is explicitly distinguished from).

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-forms.md — F-114, eighth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-114 is the next entry after F-113, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Returns to Intermediate depth after three Advanced-tier internals chapters (F-111–113).
- `handbook/frontend/react-forms.md` (F-114: controlled vs. uncontrolled, validation timing strategies, React Hook Form / Zod).
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-forms/`, 3 demo components, with real `react-hook-form` 7.85.0 + `zod` 4.4.3 (not a hand-rolled approximation). `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **Controlled vs. uncontrolled proven with a real, measured render-count contrast:** 3 individual keystrokes into a controlled field moved its render counter `2 -> 8` (+6, StrictMode-doubled); typing 3 characters into an uncontrolled field left its counter unchanged, only an explicit "Read value" click moved it `+2`.
- **A real browser-automation-tool limitation caught and worked around, not hidden:** the `computer` tool's `type` action, given a multi-character string, dispatches ONE combined input event rather than one per character — discovered because typing `"hello"` in one call produced only `+2` renders (expected `+10`). Confirmed by isolating single-character `type` calls, each reliably producing `+2`. Worked around by issuing one `type` call per character for the render-count proof; documented in the app's own README as a testing-tool artifact, not a demo or React bug.
- **Validation timing proven with a real, side-by-side contrast:** the identical min-3-characters rule applied via `onChange` (error shown instantly), `onBlur` (error shown only after focus loss), and `onSubmit` (error shown only after submit) — same input `"a"`, three genuinely different real observed moments.
- **React Hook Form + Zod proven with a real failed submit then a real successful one:** submitting invalid values (`username: "ab"`, empty email, empty age) produced all three real zod error messages simultaneously, `submitCount: 1`, `isSubmitSuccessful: false`; correcting all three fields and resubmitting produced zero errors, `submitCount: 2`, `isSubmitSuccessful: true`.
- Added a `react-forms` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5190).
- Cross-linked to `react-concurrent-rendering.md` (prerequisite, per the register's sequential convention) and `react-reconciliation-and-fiber.md` (the controlled-input re-render mechanism is a direct application of that chapter's reconciliation model).
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-error-boundaries.md — F-115, ninth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-115 is the next entry after F-114, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round).
- `handbook/frontend/react-error-boundaries.md` (F-115: error boundaries & error handling strategy).
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-error-handling/`, 3 demo components plus a real, from-scratch class-component `ErrorBoundary` (no library — error boundaries have no hook equivalent as of React 19). `npm run build` confirmed a clean production build.
- **Real render-phase crash, caught, and recovered:** incrementing a counter to a threshold triggered a genuine `throw` during render; the boundary's fallback showed the exact error message, and clicking "Reset" (key-remount) produced a genuinely fresh, recovered instance at count 0.
- **A real, measured blast-radius contrast between granular and shared boundaries:** crashing one widget under its own boundary left two sibling widgets fully intact (`B: OK`, `C: OK`); crashing the same widget under a single shared boundary replaced the ENTIRE row — the unaffected siblings were not merely hidden, they were not rendered at all.
- **Real, observed proof that error boundaries do NOT catch event-handler errors:** a `throw` inside `onClick` never triggered the wrapping boundary's fallback — a sibling paragraph stayed mounted throughout, and only a global `window` `'error'` listener captured the error (`Uncaught Error: Thrown from onClick, not from render`), directly confirming it escaped React's error-boundary mechanism. Contrasted with a correct, locally `try`/`catch`-handled version in the same demo.
- Caught and corrected a non-standard front-matter field (`official_references_note`) during drafting, per the same discipline applied in F-111 — the explanatory content was moved into prose in the Official References section instead.
- Added a `react-error-handling` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5191).
- Cross-linked to `react-forms.md` (prerequisite) and `react-reconciliation-and-fiber.md` (the fiber-tree-walk and subtree-replacement mechanism this chapter's internals section builds on directly).
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-accessibility.md — F-116, tenth frontend chapter)

- **Real accessibility-tree role contrast + real keyboard-skip proof:** a real `<button>` exposed `role: button`; an identically-styled `<div onClick>` exposed only `role: generic`. A real keyboard trace (focus the button, press Tab, check `document.activeElement`) confirmed the div was skipped entirely — never received focus — while a direct mouse click on the same div still fired its handler, proving the gap is invisible to purely visual/mouse QA.
- **Real, independently-verified focus-trap modal:** three separate behaviors each confirmed via `document.activeElement` — opening the modal moved focus to the first focusable element automatically; Tab from the last focusable wrapped back to the first (trap held, no escape to the page behind); Escape closed the modal AND returned focus to the exact trigger button, not `<body>`.
- **Real, resolved `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby` association:** typing an invalid value and blurring produced `aria-invalid="true"`, `aria-describedby="username-error"`, and resolving that id via `document.getElementById` returned the actual error text — confirmed as a real, resolvable relationship, not just visual proximity.
- Added a `react-accessibility` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5192).

Scrolling to `scrollTop=2000` and re-querying confirmed the count stayed `15` but the rendered rows shifted to `Row 68`-`Row 82`, proving a genuine moving window, not a fixed first-N cap.

### Added (handbook/frontend/react-testing.md — F-118, twelfth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-118 is the next entry after F-117, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Register flags this topic as a direct parallel to the backend `test-strategy-and-test-doubles.md` chapter.
- `handbook/frontend/react-testing.md` (F-118: React Testing Library philosophy, Jest/Vitest, mocking, Playwright/Cypress for E2E).
- **Real React 19.2.8 + Vite 8.2.1 app** — `practice/frontend/react-testing/`, with a real Vitest + React Testing Library suite (3 test files, 6 tests), a real Playwright E2E suite (2 tests against a real Chromium instance), and a clean `npm run build`.
- **Real, live-performed refactor proving the behavior-vs-implementation-detail query distinction:** `LoginForm.jsx`'s wrapper class was renamed `.field-wrap` → `.input-group` and its two fields reordered mid-session. Re-running the suite immediately after captured a real failure (`Unable to fire a "change" event - please provide a DOM element`) in the class-name/position-based test, while the role/label-based test needed zero changes — then the brittle test was updated to match the new markup, restoring a clean 3-passed/6-passed baseline.
- **Real mocking evidence:** `UserProfile.test.jsx` mocks `fetchUser` via `vi.mock`, asserting both the resulting behavior (loading → name, and loading → error-alert on rejection) and the interaction itself (`toHaveBeenCalledWith(42)`) — the same return-value-vs-interaction distinction the backend's `test-strategy-and-test-doubles.md` makes for Mockito.
- **Real Playwright E2E run, including a genuine bug it caught unscripted:** the first real run against a real installed Chromium (`npx playwright install chromium`) failed one of two tests with a real `strict mode violation: getByRole('alert') resolved to 2 elements` — `UserProfile`'s real, backend-less `fetch('/api/users/7')` genuinely rejects and renders its own `role="alert"` on the same page. Fixed by scoping the locator to the login form; re-run passed both tests.
- **Real Vitest/Playwright tooling collision, caught and fixed:** `npm run test` initially tried to execute `e2e/login.spec.js` as a Vitest test (Vitest's default include glob also matches `*.spec.js`), failing with `Playwright Test did not expect test() to be called here.` Fixed with an explicit `exclude: ['e2e/**']` in `vite.config.js`'s `test` block.
- Added a `react-testing` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5194); Playwright's own dev server runs on a separate port (5195) via `playwright.config.js`'s `webServer` config to avoid clashing with the `.claude/launch.json`-managed instance.

### Added (handbook/frontend/nextjs-fundamentals.md — F-201, fifteenth frontend chapter, opens D-F2)

- `handbook/frontend/nextjs-fundamentals.md` (F-201: Next.js's role — file-based routing, why a meta-framework over plain React/Vite).
- **Real Next.js 16.3.1 App Router app** — `practice/frontend/react-nextjs-fundamentals/`, four routes (`/`, `/about`, `/blog/[slug]`, plus the root layout) created purely by file location, zero router configuration.
- This Next.js version shipped after this assistant's training cutoff: before writing any route code, `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/03-layouts-and-pages.md` (the version's own bundled docs, flagged via its auto-generated `AGENTS.md`) was read directly to confirm current App Router conventions — notably that `params` in a dynamic route is a `Promise` requiring `await`, not a plain object.
- **Real captured route manifest:** `npm run build` produced a real `next build` route table (`○ /`, `○ /about`, `ƒ /blog/[slug]`) derived purely from file locations in `app/`, with no router import or route registration anywhere in the project.
- **Real, measured layout-persistence evidence:** a `useRef`-based mount counter inside the root layout's `PersistentHeader.js` stayed unchanged across three real `<Link>`-driven navigations (Home → About → Blog), while each page's own content changed on every transition.
- **Real Navigation Timing API confirmation:** `performance.getEntriesByType('navigation').length === 1` after three page transitions, confirming those transitions were genuinely client-side, not disguised full reloads.
- **Real dynamic-segment resolution:** the same `app/blog/[slug]/page.js` file correctly resolved two different real URLs (`/blog/hello-world` → `params.slug = "hello-world"`, `/blog/file-based-routing` → `params.slug = "file-based-routing"`).
- Added a `react-nextjs-fundamentals` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 5198).
- Cross-linked to `react-state-management.md` (prerequisite, closes D-F1).

### Added (handbook/frontend/nextjs-server-vs-client-components.md — F-203, seventeenth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-203 is the next entry after F-202, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Register flags this as "the single most-tested modern Next.js concept."
- `handbook/frontend/nextjs-server-vs-client-components.md` (F-203: Server Components vs. Client Components — the actual boundary, `"use client"`, what runs where).
- **Extended the SAME real Next.js 16.3.1 app again** (`practice/frontend/react-nextjs-fundamentals/`) — added `app/components/ServerSecretDemo.js` (a Server Component reading a server-only env var), `app/components/ClientCounter.js` (a Client Component with real `useState`/`onClick`), and `app/server-vs-client/page.js` rendering both. Added `.env.local` (gitignored, not committed) with a fake `SERVER_SECRET_DEMO` value.
- **Real grep-verified server-secret code/output distinction:** the secret string appeared exactly once in the real prerendered HTML (`.next/server/app/server-vs-client.html`) and zero times across all 15 files under the real client bundle directory (`.next/static/`) — direct proof a Server Component's code never ships to the client even though its rendered output does.
- **Real captured build error:** adding `useState` to `ServerSecretDemo.js` (a Server Component) produced a real `next build` error naming the exact file and import trace — reverted after capture.
- **A genuine, version-specific finding, verified live rather than assumed from pre-cutoff knowledge:** marking `ClientCounter` `async function` was expected to fail `next build`; it did not — the real build succeeded. The restriction (`<ClientCounter> is an async Client Component. Only Server Components can be async at the moment.`) only surfaced as a real runtime console error and a full Next.js dev error overlay once the component was actually rendered and clicked in a browser. Reverted, then re-verified real click-to-re-render interactivity (`Client count: 0` → `Client count: 1`) on a clean production `next build` + `next start` server.
- Updated `react-nextjs-fundamentals`'s own README.md (shared by F-201/F-202/F-203) with the new captured evidence; no `.claude/launch.json` change needed (same app, same port 5198).
- Cross-linked to `nextjs-app-router-fundamentals.md` (prerequisite); added a forward cross-link from that chapter's Additional Reading to this chapter.

### Added (handbook/frontend/nextjs-data-fetching-and-caching.md — F-204, eighteenth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-204 is the next entry after F-203, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round).
- `handbook/frontend/nextjs-data-fetching-and-caching.md` (F-204: `fetch` caching semantics, `revalidate`, `cache: 'no-store'`).
- Confirmed directly against this Next.js version's own bundled docs that the app runs under the "Previous Model" of caching (`cacheComponents` not enabled in `next.config.mjs`) — exactly the API surface the register's F-204 topic names; the newer, opt-in "Cache Components" model (`"use cache"`, `cacheLife`) was noted but scoped out as a separate, later concern.
- **Extended the SAME real Next.js 16.3.1 app again** (`practice/frontend/react-nextjs-fundamentals/`) — added four `app/data-fetching/*/page.js` routes (default, `no-store`, `force-cache`, `revalidate`), `app/actions.js` (a real Server Action calling `revalidateTag`), and `app/components/RevalidateButton.js` (a real Client Component invoking it).
- **A real, discovered build failure and fix:** the first build attempt fetched a same-server API route from the `force-cache` demo and failed with a real captured `ECONNREFUSED` — `force-cache` fetches are attempted AT BUILD TIME, before the app's own server exists. Fixed by switching all four demos to a real external endpoint (`https://httpbin.org/uuid`) reachable during the build; the now-unnecessary same-server API route was removed.
- **A real, nuanced central finding:** real `curl` evidence against a clean `next start` production server showed a default (no-`cache`-option) fetch returning the SAME value on two real requests — behaving identically to explicit `force-cache`, NOT like explicit `cache: 'no-store'` (which alone produced genuinely different values per request). Confirmed independently by the real build's own route manifest (`default` marked `○` Static, `no-store` marked `ƒ` Dynamic).
- **Real, timed proof of `next: { revalidate: 5 }`:** stable across real requests at t=0s/1s/2s (within the window), changed after a real 7-second sleep crossed the boundary, then stable again immediately afterward.
- **Real, zero-wait proof of on-demand `revalidateTag`:** a real click on the live `RevalidateButton` in a browser session changed the `force-cache` page's cached value immediately, with no timer involved, then re-stabilized on the new value.
- Updated `react-nextjs-fundamentals`'s own README.md (shared by F-201/F-202/F-203/F-204) with the new captured evidence; no `.claude/launch.json` change needed (same app, same port 5198).
- Cross-linked to `nextjs-server-vs-client-components.md` (prerequisite) and the backend's `../system-design/caching-strategies-and-invalidation.md`; added a forward cross-link from `nextjs-server-vs-client-components.md`'s Additional Reading to this chapter.
- All cross-links and heading structure (1 H1) verified before commit.

### Added (handbook/frontend/nextjs-rendering-strategies.md — F-205, nineteenth frontend chapter)
- Continued the frontend register in sequence (F-205 is the next entry after F-204, no ambiguity, proceeded without an `AskUserQuestion` round). Register frames this as "a direct analogue to the backend's caching-strategies cheat sheet."

Deliberately does NOT re-demonstrate ISR — cites F-204's already-proven `revalidate`/`revalidateTag` evidence directly rather than duplicating it; builds new real evidence only for SSR and SSG, the two strategies F-204 didn't cover.
- **Extended the SAME real Next.js 16.3.1 app again** (`practice/frontend/react-nextjs-fundamentals/`) — added `app/rendering-strategies/ssr/page.js` (reads `headers()`, forcing genuine SSR) and `app/rendering-strategies/ssg/[id]/page.js` (`generateStaticParams()` returning `["1", "2"]`).
- **A real, third build-manifest marker discovered:** the real build output marked `generateStaticParams`-driven routes `●` (SSG) — a symbol distinct from the `○` (Static) marker prior chapters' plain routes received — direct, build-tool-level confirmation SSG is its own mechanical category.
- **Real per-request SSR proof:** `curl` with two different `User-Agent` headers against a clean `next start` server rendered two genuinely different values back, confirming per-request server execution (and independently confirmed `ƒ` in the manifest).
- **Real build-time-frozen SSG proof, plus real on-demand fallback:** `id=1` (listed) returned an identical render timestamp across two requests (build-time-fixed); `id=999` (unlisted) returned a different, later timestamp than `id=1` but an identical timestamp across its OWN two requests — real proof of on-demand generation on first request, then caching, not a 404.
- Updated `react-nextjs-fundamentals`'s own README.md (shared by F-201 through F-205) with the new captured evidence; no `.claude/launch.json` change needed (same app, same port 5198).
- Cross-linked to `nextjs-data-fetching-and-caching.md` (prerequisite) and the backend's `../system-design/caching-strategies-and-invalidation.md`; added a forward cross-link from `nextjs-data-fetching-and-caching.md`'s Additional Reading to this chapter.
